# Deploy Log — `dev` branch / Railway Worker

Running log of pushes to `dev` that trigger a Railway redeploy. Each push
restarts the Worker container, which causes a 1–3 minute gap in bar
recording (warmup re-runs, no live alerts during restart). Use this log
to correlate cache gaps, alert misses, or WebSocket reconnect events
with deploy windows rather than chasing them as data bugs.

Times are recorded in **UTC** (chart timestamps) and **MT** (Kevin's
local). Worker container restart time = ~30s build + ~30–60s warmup =
~1–2 min unavailable per deploy.

## Format

```
- **HH:MM UTC (HH:MM MT)** — `<short_sha>` <commit subject>
  - Service(s) redeployed: Worker / api / frontend / streamlit
  - Observed cache gap: <bar_start times affected, or "none observed">
  - Notes: anything unusual (stuck deploys, reverts, etc.)
```

  - ⭐ **THE CAR: `Staged` STOPPED MEANING "SHIPPED".** The ship sweep (Wave 25's #242) ticked a car's *step* and left its *status* alone, so shipped work sat in `Staged` — *"reviewed, has code, waiting for a release train"* — forever, and every count built on `status` inherited the lie. On 07-30 the dispatch page's STAGED tile read **7** under that caption while **five of the seven had already shipped**, and Kevin read the tile against an empty shipping lane and asked why they disagreed. This car advances the status on the **same `git merge-base --is-ancestor` proof the tick already required** — chain finished → `Done`, anything still open → `Review`, and **never `Closed`** (that is Kevin's own status, and a tool that can write it can manufacture his sign-off). Both writes are attributed with a `status: X -> Y (by <actor>)` system comment, and the two-touch guard (#136) is re-applied in the tool because the status write must go over PostgREST — `PATCH /api/dev-tasks/<id>` is user-gated and 401s for a service key (#217).
  - ⭐ **AND IT PROVED ITSELF ON ITS OWN CAR, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, WITHIN FOUR MINUTES.** This is the rare train whose cargo can be exercised end-to-end by the train itself, so it was — and the **negative** control was taken first. **Pre-merge**, the sweep was run from the gated tree and **refused**: *"`fix/ship-sweep-status-249` is NOT an ancestor of origin/dev — it has not shipped, so its ship step stays open"*. **Post-merge**, the same script from the same tree: **`OK #249 step 4 "Ship via a release train" ticked by R-A·auto · handed off to kevin · status Staged -> Review`**, exit **0**. The board was then re-read **independently of the tool's own report** — `#249 status='Review' assignee='kevin'` — and the sweep **re-run**, which now sees `0 shipped` and does not list #249 at all: **idempotent, as designed**. The `Review` target (not `Done`) is correct: #249's chain still has open steps after its ship step.

The sweep is a script agents run **from a checkout**, not something the api or Worker serves. So the green rollup below proves the seven services survived a redeploy of unchanged code — worth having, but **the proof that this car works is the paragraph above**, not the deploy. Recording this because a reader scanning for "deploy CLEAN 7/7" would otherwise credit it with something it did not test.

All six other suites were then **re-run under the venv too** and were green there as well, so the whole set is proved interpreter-independent rather than merely proved-once. The trap is worth naming precisely: a suite that dies at import is **not** a red gate about the change, and a run that stopped at the exit code without reading *why* would have aborted this train over a missing package.

It was bisected by restoring the file and re-running suites one at a time: **`src/test_pytest_false_green_guard_239.py` is the writer** (it imports `conftest.py`, which loads the pack registry, which regenerates the config); `test_shipping_lane_unmerged_branch_245.py` and `test_car_ship_tick.py` each leave the tree **clean**. It is **pre-existing and not this train's doing**, and it was **discarded, never committed** — this train's branch is `Deploy_Log.md` and nothing else, verified by `git diff --stat origin/dev..HEAD`. But the shape is dangerous: **any agent that runs that suite and then commits with `git add -A` sweeps 241 unintended config lines into its car**, and the file is engine configuration.
  - ⭐ **HOW THIS CAR WAS FOUND — 52 SECONDS, BY THE #255 CADENCE, WITH NO HUMAN IN THE PATH.** M approved and staged #249 at **06:11:02Z**. No train followed, because `brief_gen.py` refused it. R's watcher flagged **`CARS WAITING FOR A TRAIN = 1` at 06:11:54Z** — **52 seconds later** — and R hand-assembled this train. That is the same class of stall that cost **four hours** on #246 (`Staged` is terminal; the dispatcher never dispatches out of it, so work parks there silently), caught here in under a minute. The R session exists for exactly this, and this is its cleanest catch so far.

The asymmetry is now visible **inside this one directory**: this train's own `car_ship_tick.py` declares `SHIP_OWNERS = ("R", "R-A")` and found #249's step 4 without trouble, five lines of code away from the rule that cannot. Until `brief_gen` is fixed, **every train must be hand-authored, and any car M does not personally arm will strand silently.** R has raised this for M; it is not this train's cargo.

- **05:03:49 UTC 07-31 (23:03 MT 07-30)** — `573d5d3f` **Wave-33 train — ONE car, FIVE files, 23 suites / 1,303 checks green (13 briefed, run twice byte-identical, + 10 volunteered), deploy CLEAN 7/7 — and the first train whose NEW API BEHAVIOUR was proved live in prod by two no-write `400`s, not inferred from the deployed SHA.** (board #267, R-A·auto). ✅ **Market CLOSED** — merged **9h04m after the 20:00Z close** (the log keeps this under the 07-30 heading because it is 23:03 **MT** on 07-30; the UTC date has rolled to 07-31). Live-trade **DORMANT** (no money at risk). PR **#201** `feat/goal-task-type-262` @ `dbc4c2ea` → merge **`573d5d3f`**, the final dev head. Whole-wave diff `b4f21bb4`..`origin/dev` = **5 files, 1,247 insertions / 18 deletions**. Backup branch `backup/dev-pre-wave33-0731` pushed at `b4f21bb4` and verified on the remote (`git ls-remote` → `b4f21bb455e13d97…`) before the merge. **Zero conflicts** — a clean fast-forward onto the gated base.
  - ⭐ **THE CAR: THE `goal` TYPE STOPS BEING A LABEL AND GAINS ITS RULES AND ITS OUTPUT.** Wave 32 shipped `task_type` as a column with `goal ⚑` in the vocabulary and `goal_params` as a JSONB column **read by nothing**. This train makes both do work: a **`goal_params` editor** (new ⚑ Goal tab, rendered from a declarative `GOAL_PARAM_FIELDS`, shown only when `task_type === 'goal'`, requireds outlined red when blank) · the **context block** that starts a goal session against Claude's built-in `/goal` · **autonomy wired to the EXISTING `standing_approval` column** · and a **nesting guard**. Net effect: the board can now carry a container that starts its own session. **The next goal-related step is Kevin authoring a real one** — the machinery is complete and untested by use, which is exactly what step 3 of #267 exists to do.
  - 🔬 **THE REFUSAL IS THE DESIGN, AND IT IS TOTAL.** `renderGoalBlock` returns **no block at all** (`block: null`, `ok: false`) when `done_when` is blank — *"a goal with no observable end condition never ends, so the block that starts one will not render without it"* — because a half-rendered block is the one that gets pasted anyway. Enforced at **RENDER** time, deliberately **not** in the API: the API validates `goal_params` **shape only** (object or null), so a half-authored goal can still be **saved**. That split keeps #261's *"no API-level status enforcement in this arc"* fence intact and leaves required-field enforcement to #168.
  - ✅ **AUTONOMY USES THE COLUMN THAT ALREADY EXISTED — no parallel field.** `standing_approval` had been inert; it now means something, and a stray `goal_params.autonomy` **loses to the column and is stripped on write**. The source says so in capitals so the next author cannot re-introduce the duplicate. Children inherit unless `goal_params.autonomy_override` overrides, and the modal shows a child what it inherited.
  - ⭐ **THE NEW API GUARDS WERE PROVED LIVE IN PROD BY TWO REFUSALS THAT WROTE NOTHING — the check Wave 32 explicitly recorded as *not* verified.** `_validate_team_fields` is the first thing the PATCH handler calls after `_admin()`, **before any write**, so a `400` from it is a pure read of what code is loaded. Against prod, authenticated, targeting this train's own task #267: `{"goal_params": "not-an-object"}` → **`400` `"goal_params must be an object (or null) — send the WHOLE object; JSONB is replaced, not merged"`** · `{"task_type": "goal", "parent_id": 999999}` → **`400` `"a goal is a top-level container — it cannot be filed under #999999 (not under a vision, and not under another goal). Clear parent_id, or make it a vision."`** Both messages **verbatim from the merged source**, neither reachable in the pre-merge build (`goal_params` was not in `_EDITABLE`, so the old code would have stripped it and returned `200` unchanged). #267 was then re-read: `task_type=action`, `parent_id=None`, `goal_params=None`, status and assignee unmoved — **the probe mutated nothing.** Wave 32 could not do this because it probed unauthenticated and was refused at auth before validation (#217); the fix was to mint a JWT from the `src/.env` test account, which is the documented path.

The design point is written into the doc as an asymmetry: *the watcher is cheap and stays up; the session is expensive and wakes only on signal*, and **silence is the correct output** — a watcher that narrates quiet polls becomes noise and gets ignored, which defeats it. This is the behavioural half of what Wave 28 shipped the surfaces for: Wave 28 built `/admin/r-session` and the opener, Wave 29 says **when R is allowed to be asleep.**

The PR read `OPEN`, so R-A retried — and every retry for the next ~6 minutes returned **`GraphQL: Merge already in progress (mergePullRequest)`**, i.e. GitHub had accepted the *first* call and was holding a lock, while `gh pr view` still reported `state=OPEN merged=null`. R-A stopped issuing merge calls and polled state alone for ~6½ minutes (20 samples), then re-read mergeability (`MERGEABLE`/`CLEAN`) and issued one final merge, which landed at **20:32:08Z**. **The lesson for the next release agent: a 502 from `gh pr merge` does NOT mean the merge did not happen.** Retrying into "merge already in progress" is harmless here but wasted ~8 minutes; the right move is to poll `state` until it settles before re-issuing. Net effect on this train: car 3 landed **8m49s** after cars 1–2, so `dev` briefly carried two of three cars.

  - **ROOT CAUSE — read out of the api logs, not inferred:** `File "/app/src/api/routers/m_session.py", line 66, in <module> / _REPO_ROOT = _ROR_ROOT.parents[2] / IndexError: 2`. `create_app()` imports the m_session router, the router raised **at module scope**, and uvicorn therefore never loaded the app at all. #220 derived a monorepo root by walking **two levels above** the RoR_Trader root — correct locally (`.../Kevbot-Toolkit/clients/KevBot_Toolkit/RoR_Trader`), **impossible in the container**, where `Dockerfile.api` sets `WORKDIR /app` and `_ROR_ROOT` resolves to `/app`, whose only parent is `/`.
  - ⭐ **THE ESCAPE HATCH WAS ALREADY THERE AND WAS UNREACHABLE.** `RORT_RULES_ROOT` / `RORT_REPO_ROOT` existed on lines 84–85 with a comment naming this exact case — *"the deployed API, whose working copy may not carry the whole monorepo."* But the fallback was computed **eagerly on line 66** and raised **before** the overrides could be read on line 74. The module anticipated the deployed layout and then crashed on the way to handling it. The fix wraps that one computation in `try/except IndexError` and falls back to `_ROR_ROOT`; local resolution is byte-identical.
  - 🔬 **THE GATE THAT WOULD HAVE CAUGHT IT, AND NOW EXISTS AS A PROCEDURE:** every one of Wave 24's 21 suites passed, and `build` was green on PR #173 — **all of them on a filesystem where the path arithmetic works.** Nothing exercised the container layout. Before merging this hotfix R-A exec'd the module with `__file__` set to the **exact path from the traceback** (`/app/src/api/routers/m_session.py`): the pre-fix source from `origin/dev` reproduced the identical `line 66 / IndexError: 2`, and the fixed source imported with `_REPO_ROOT=/app`. **A path constant derived from `__file__` is not covered by a test that runs from the repo.**

  - 🚨 **THE HEADLINE: `api` CRASHES AT IMPORT AND THE GREEN GATES COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT IT, BECAUSE THE BUG IS A PROPERTY OF THE CONTAINER'S FILESYSTEM DEPTH, NOT OF THE CODE'S BEHAVIOUR.** Car 6 (#220) adds `src/api/routers/m_session.py`, which `api/main.py:83` imports at `create_app()` time. Line 66 of that router reads `_REPO_ROOT = _ROR_ROOT.parents[2]`. Locally the file sits at `…/Kevbot-Toolkit/clients/KevBot_Toolkit/RoR_Trader/src/api/routers/m_session.py`, so `parents[2]` resolves. **In the Railway container the app root is `/app`**, so the same expression walks off the top of the path and raises. Verbatim, from `railway logs --service api`:
    ```
    File "/app/src/api/main.py", line 219, in <module>
        app = create_app()
    File "/app/src/api/main.py", line 83, in create_app
        from api.routers.m_session import router as m_session_router
    File "/app/src/api/routers/m_session.py", line 66, in <module>
        _REPO_ROOT = _ROR_ROOT.parents[2]
    IndexError: 2
    ```
    Uvicorn never finishes `config.load_app()`, so the process exits before binding — which is why the edge serves **502**, not a 500 from inside the app.
  - ⭐ **THE ESCAPE HATCH FOR EXACTLY THIS WAS WRITTEN, AND IT IS UNREACHABLE — this is the precise defect, and it matters for whoever fixes it.** Six lines below the crash, the author anticipated this case explicitly: *"`RORT_RULES_ROOT` / `RORT_REPO_ROOT` exist for the deployed API, whose working copy may not carry the whole monorepo"*, followed by `_REPO_ROOT = Path(os.getenv("RORT_REPO_ROOT") or _REPO_ROOT)`. But the unguarded `parents[2]` on **line 66** executes *before* the override on **line 72** can apply. **Consequence: setting `RORT_REPO_ROOT` on the api service will NOT fix this.** The env var can only be consulted by code that is never reached. The fix has to be a code change (a bounded/guarded parent lookup, or reading the env var first) or a rollback of car 6 — there is no configuration-only remedy. Recording this because "just set the env var" is the obvious next move and it would burn a deploy cycle proving it does nothing.

---

## How to use this log

When investigating a cache gap, alert miss, or WebSocket reconnect:

1. Note the affected timestamp window from the data.
2. Check this log for any entry within ±5 min of that window.
3. If a deploy lines up, the gap is expected and you can move on.
4. If no deploy lines up, dig deeper — it's a real issue.

When you (Claude) push code that triggers a Worker / api / frontend
redeploy, append a new entry at the **top** of the most recent date
section before reporting back to Kevin. Pull the commit SHA and time
from `git log` after pushing. Notes about observed gaps can be added
later once validated.
